# PYQ Vault — project notes

**Brand:** the product is **PYQ Vault**, live at **https://www.pyqvault.com** (rebranded from "Question Bank" 2026-06-04 — the repo/folder/Vercel-project are still named `question-bank`; in code the brand is `SITE_NAME` in `layout.tsx`). Lowercase "question bank" in prose still means the question corpus, not the brand.

Free, public past-year-question paper builder for Indian entrance exams. Anyone can filter the public bank by exam/chapter/subtopic/difficulty/PYQ year, then download a Question Paper + Answer Key as Word files. Admins (org members) upload Excel batches privately, then flip individual questions PUBLIC. Sibling app `MHT_CET_AI` posts finalized mocks to `/api/sync/mock` for cross-app dedup. Currently MHT-CET + NDA + **JEE Mains** (2021 — **718 q PUBLIC**: standard shift Papers 1-10 + 18, plus compilation Paper 11; Paper 12 staged PRIVATE; ingested via the `scripts/jee/` pandoc pipeline — see [[jee-mains-ingestion]] for the per-paper state + resume point) + **CDS English** (new exam; **ALL 19 papers ingested PRIVATE — 2017-I … 2026-I = 2,280 q**; scanned booklets, **no answer key** → LLM-derived + confidence-flagged answers awaiting human spot-check before PUBLIC; `scripts/cds/` pipeline + per-exam dedup migration 0038 — see [[cds-english-ingestion]] + [[section-based-exam-ingestion]]); IPMAT, CUET, NEET on the roadmap.

(MHT-CET Chemistry dropped 1,553 → 1,529 → **1,525** on 2026-06-03 — first 24 exact-duplicate rows deduped, then during the source-PDF verification pass: 1 OF₂ conflicting-key duplicate deleted + 3 unreconstructable broken-extraction rows set PRIVATE; 6 image-naming rows + 5 conflicting-key pairs remain deferred. MHT-CET Physics dropped 1,577 → 1,574 on 2026-06-02 — 3 cross-shift duplicate rows deduped during its solution-cleanup. Admin "Publish to public" on `/dashboard/quizzes` sets `public_slug`; leads roll up by mobile at `/dashboard/leads`; `/privacy` for DPDP consent; a "Test yourself" CTA on `/notes`. **Dormant until an admin Publishes a quiz.** See the 2026-06-09 Decisions log + [[project-public-quiz-funnel]].

## Stack

- **Next.js 14** App Router · TypeScript · Tailwind 3 · shadcn-style UI primitives
- **Supabase** Auth + Postgres + RLS, accessed via `@supabase/supabase-js` (no Prisma, no ORM)
- **Vitest 2** for tests · `tsx` for scripts
- **Word export:** `temml` (LaTeX → MathML) → `mathml2omml` → `docx` + `jszip`
- **Browser math preview:** `katex` + `react-katex`
- **Deploy:** Vercel, git-integrated

## Multi-tenancy + visibility model

Per-school orgs, RLS-enforced. Three tiers of access:
- **Service role** (seed scripts, server-only admin actions, `/api/admin/members`) bypasses RLS by design — never expose to client code.
- **ADMIN** can do everything in own org: insert + update + delete questions, write options, upload Excel batches, auto-create chapters/subtopics, flip visibility, triage reports, manage members.
- **TEACHER** (as of migration 0025 / 2026-05-26) can **edit** question content — text, options, taxonomy moves, difficulty, PYQ metadata, images, concept/principle tags, leave-set — in own org. Cannot upload Excel, delete questions, flip PUBLIC/PRIVATE, auto-create chapters/subtopics, triage reports, or manage members. Server-side guard in `applyEdit` rejects `payload.visibility` changes from TEACHER (`kind: "forbidden_field"` → HTTP 403) so a tampered request can't bypass the hidden UI toggle.
- **anon** (no JWT) can read PUBLIC questions/options + all taxonomy. Cannot write anything.
- **STUDENT** (signed-in, self-serve, **no `org_members` row** — added 2026-06-01) registers at `/signup` (Google OAuth or email/password). By RLS construction they get zero private data; what an account unlocks is in-app question reports today + premium content via the entitlement axis below. Org-less = student (admins always get an `org_members` row atomically), so `/dashboard` redirects them to `/browse`.

**Premium entitlement axis (orthogonal to orgs, migration 0026).** Paid/comped access lives in `public.entitlements`, keyed on `auth.users(id)` — NOT org-scoped, because a student has no org. A grant unlocks a `scope` ('all' = full premium); `source` ∈ `razorpay | comp | manual` (the gate is **source-agnostic** — `userHasAccess` just asks "active entitlement for this scope?"; staff/org-members bypass the paywall). Writes are **service-role only** (no write RLS); reads are own-row only. Consumers: the `/notes` preview-gate (`tier:"paid"` chapters), `/pricing`/`/account`, the Razorpay webhook, and the `/dashboard/entitlements` comp UI. See the 2026-06-01 Decisions log entry + [[project-paywall-build]].

Each question has a `visibility` enum (`PUBLIC | PRIVATE`, default **PUBLIC** as of migration 0022 / 2026-05-17 — was PRIVATE prior). Read RLS is the union of two permissive policies: `(visibility = 'PUBLIC')` for everyone, plus `(org_id = private.current_user_org_id())` for authenticated org members on their own org's PRIVATE rows. Authenticated users see PUBLIC across all orgs + their own PRIVATE; anon sees only PUBLIC. Admins flip per-question visibility from the edit page.

Each question also has a **`question_kind` enum** (`pyq | practice`, default `pyq`, migration 0036) — the axis that separates the past-year corpus from the supplementary **practice** bank (the product is PYQ-first; **3,040 practice q live across 29 NDA Maths chapters as of 2026-06-13 — ALL 5 source folders ingested (Algebra + Trigonometry + Calculus + 2D Geometry + 3D Geometry; the 3D folder fed both the Vectors and 3D Geometry chapters)**, separate from the PYQ counts above). `/browse` exposes it as a **PYQ / Practice / All** toggle (default PYQ; `kind` filter in [filters.ts](src/lib/questions/filters.ts) → `query.ts` + `p_kind` on the facet RPCs). PYQ-only surfaces stay clean two ways: **tag-gated** surfaces (guide principles, notes drills, worked examples, quiz harvest) are PYQ-only *by construction* (practice questions are never principle/concept-tagged — doing so would leak them in), while the four **raw count-by-taxonomy** queries (`examHomeStats` + the 3 notes landings) carry an explicit `question_kind='pyq'` guard. Practice ingestion is its own pipeline + runbook — see [[practice-ingestion]] + `scripts/practice/README.md`.

RLS helpers live in the **`private` schema** (not exposed by PostgREST): `private.current_user_org_id()`, `private.current_user_is_admin()`, `private.current_user_can_edit_questions()`. New org-scoped policies should reference these. Use `current_user_is_admin()` for destructive / privileged ops (DELETE, upload, visibility flip, reports triage, member management); use `current_user_can_edit_questions()` for content edits that TEACHER should be allowed to do.

## File layout

The full file-by-file map — the `src/` tree (routes, components, lib helpers), `supabase/` migrations + seed, `scripts/`, and `tests/` — lives in **[ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md)**, extracted to keep this file's always-loaded payload lean. **Append a new file/component/route there, not here**; update the high-level shape below only when the top-level structure changes.

High-level shape:
- `src/app/` — Next.js App Router pages: `/browse` (paper builder), `/guide` (10 NDA strategy guides), `/notes` (cross-exam index → `/notes/<examSlug>` per-exam hubs → 20 teaching-notes chapters), `/dashboard` (admin), `/upload`, `/signup`·`/login`·`/pricing`·`/account`, plus the `/api/*` route handlers.
- `src/lib/` — server/pure helpers by domain: `supabase/`, `auth*`, `questions/`, `guide/`, `notes/`, `links/`, `export/` (Word/OMML), `entitlements/`, `billing/`, `reports/`+`notes-reports/`, `upload/`, `tags/`, `quiz/` (the **Quiz-Factory core**: `atoms`/`daily`/`quizPayload`/`stemLint`/`verdict`/`coverage` pure helpers + `assemble.ts` shared `assembleNextQuiz` (snapshots questions onto the quiz row) + `deleteQuiz.ts` (delete + propagate to nda-tracker) + `admin.ts` dashboard reads — the `scripts/quiz/` CLIs import these; `coverage.ts` powers `quiz:coverage`).
- `src/components/` — `AppHeader`/nav, `math/` renderers (`KatexRenderer`·`RichText`·`BlockText`·`parseTableBlocks`), shadcn `ui/` primitives.
- `supabase/migrations/` — `0001…0040` (append-only; 0040 = **RAG grounding layer** — `pgvector` (extensions schema) + `questions.plain_text`/`solution_json`/`derived_model`/`derived_at` + generalized `embeddings` table (unsized `vector`, no index) + `match_chunks` RPC (JOIN-guarded PUBLIC+pyq); see the 2026-06-19 Decisions entry + [[rag-grounding-layer]]; 0039 = **collaborative paper builder** — `papers` + `paper_questions` junction, org-scoped RLS, finalize-snapshot; 0038 = **per-exam dedup** — swap the `questions` unique key from `(org_id, content_hash)` to `(org_id, exam_id, content_hash)` so CDS↔NDA shared UPSC questions keep both copies; 0029 = `subtopics.order_index` for /notes teaching order; 0030 = `quiz_atoms` Quiz-Factory pool; 0031 = `quizzes` + `quiz_atoms_map`; 0032 = `quiz_atoms.theme`; 0033 = `quizzes.public_slug` + `quiz_leads` (public-quiz funnel); 0034 = `record_quiz_lead` retake-aware upsert RPC; 0035 = `quizzes.questions` jsonb + `theme` (immutable quiz snapshot — display decoupled from the live atom pool); 0036 = `questions.question_kind` enum (`pyq`|`practice`); 0037 = `p_kind` on the facet RPCs for the /browse PYQ/Practice toggle). `scripts/` — seeds, `notes-lint`, `notes-latex-audit`, `notes:order` sync, `jee/` ingestion, `practice/` (NDA practice-question ingestion — `render` → vision-transcribe → `commit` → `preview`; runbook in `scripts/practice/README.md`, pure core in `lib.ts`), `quiz/` (Daily Quiz Factory CLI runners — `quiz:harvest` → `quiz:sync` → `quiz:verify` → `quiz:lint` → `quiz:assemble` → `quiz:delete` → `quiz:push`; pure core lives in `src/lib/quiz/`), `grounding/` (RAG grounding + key-audit pipeline — `backfill-plain-text` → `export-batch` → [blind agents] → `commit` → `apply-fix`; pure core `lib.ts`, runbook `README.md` — see [[rag-grounding-layer]]). `tests/` — Vitest unit + DB-integration. The `/dashboard/quizzes` admin page (read-only: pool stats + filterable list + Publish-to-public; assembly/delete are CLI-only) + `/dashboard/leads` (quiz-lead rollup) + the public `/quiz/[slug]` funnel are the UI over this.

## Commands

```sh
npm run dev                # next dev (local)
npm run build              # production build
npm run lint
npm run typecheck          # tsc --noEmit (covers test files too — keep this clean)
npm test                   # vitest run
npm run test:watch
npm run db:seed            # idempotent taxonomy seed (service-role)
npm run extract:taxonomy   # regenerate supabase/seed/taxonomy.json from a reference Excel
npm run notes:lint         # validate /notes editorial modules against live taxonomy + DB tags (read-only)
npm run notes:latex        # audit /notes for LaTeX-in-plain-text leaks + delimiter imbalance + unicode (read-only)
npm run notes:order        # populate subtopics.order_index from each /notes chapter's subtopicOrder (teaching order on /browse); --dry previews
npm run notes:coverage [subjectRoute] [chapterSlug]  # triage probe: math in a chapter's PUBLIC solutions but NOT in its notes (formula-gap candidates); default nda-maths statistics. Noisy — human-review list, not a gate
npm run tags:audit         # principle-tag audit (read-only)
npm run audit:underlines   # scan PUBLIC questions for \underline{...} segments not covered by the docx-export bypass
# — Daily Quiz Factory (see Decisions log 2026-06-08 + [[daily-quiz-pilot]] memory) —
npm run quiz:harvest [route/chapter ...]   # /notes ConceptUnits → MCQ atoms JSON (scripts/quiz/atoms/*.json); default 3 sample chapters
npm run quiz:sync          # upsert harvested atoms → public.quiz_atoms (staleness-aware: preserves verified, re-flags changed)
npm run quiz:verify [name] # promote needs_review atoms → 'verified' from scripts/quiz/verify/<name>.ts (Claude-authored distractors)
npm run quiz:lint [route] [chapter]   # flag quiz-atom stems that aren't self-contained (back-refs, criterion-less "which is correct?", fragments, generic auto-harvest "formula for <X>?" template) — paginates the full ready pool; triage, run before publishing a quiz public
npm run quiz:coverage [route] [chapter] [--review]   # flag formulas/traps TAUGHT in /notes prose but not harvestable (formula.latex empty / <12 traps) — the theme-coverage guardrail; triage, not a gate
npm run quiz:assemble <route> <chapter> [max] [size] [-- --theme=X]   # ready atoms → quiz → record (quizzes+map) → push nda-tracker draft
npm run quiz:delete <slug>   # delete an assembled quiz here + propagate the delete to nda-tracker (drafts only there)
npm run quiz:push [daily/<slug>]   # push a single DraftQuiz module to nda-tracker (default: the hand-authored classical quiz)
npm run stats              # report live bank size (PUBLIC by exam/subject) + taxonomy + /notes chapter & diagram counts — for verifying the header lines instead of hand-maintaining them
npm run db:types           # supabase gen types (requires SUPABASE_PROJECT_REF env)
npm run prepush            # the chained gate, MIRRORS CI: typecheck → lint → notes:lint → notes:latex → test → build; run by .githooks/pre-push
npm run gate [target]      # wrapper that runs `npm run <target>` (default prepush) and prints a GATE_RESULT: PASS/FAIL sentinel as its LAST line — so the result survives `| tail` even though a pipe masks the exit code
```

> **Pre-push gate.** `.githooks/pre-push` runs `npm run prepush` before any push — which now **mirrors CI exactly**: typecheck → lint → notes:lint → notes:latex → **test** → build (single source of truth: the `prepush` script). So a green pre-push means a green CI. Widened 2026-06-02 from the old build-only chain after a broken test passed pre-push and only failed in CI (it didn't run `npm test`). Cost: ~3–4 min (full suite + build). Activated by the `prepare` script (`git config core.hooksPath .githooks`) on `npm install`. `.gitattributes` pins LF on `.githooks/*` so the shebang resolves on Linux/WSL contributors. Escape hatch for WIP pushes: `git push --no-verify` — don't reach for it unless you know why.

> **CI gate.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs the **full** gate (typecheck → lint → `notes:lint` → `notes:latex` → `npm test` → build) on every push to `main` + every PR. The local pre-push hook now runs this same chain (see Pre-push gate above), so CI is a backstop rather than the first place these checks run — it still matters because it runs on a clean checkout with repo secrets even if someone `--no-verify`s. Requires three repo secrets (`NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`); without them the build + DB steps fail and the DB tests skip. Runs against the single prod Supabase project (test rows auto-swept by `global-teardown`) — point the secrets at a staging project if write traffic gets noisy. Vercel still owns deploys; CI is the correctness gate.

## Conventions specific to this project

- **Spacing scale.** Page wrappers `p-8`, content cards (CardContent default) `p-6`, compact cards (FilterBar etc.) `p-4`, nested option blocks `p-3`. Pick the smallest tier that contains breathing room — don't mix `p-4`/`p-6` in the same visual rhythm.
- **Typography.** UI text uses `font-sans` (Inter, default). Question/option/solution body text uses `font-serif` (Source Serif 4) — it's how teachers tell content apart from chrome. Both are loaded via `next/font` in `src/app/layout.tsx` and exposed as Tailwind utilities.
- **Color / brand (indigo, 2026-06-01).** The brand accent is **indigo** — chosen to unify with the `/notes` content palette. Three CSS vars in `globals.css`: `--brand` + `--brand-foreground` (fills — indigo-600 + white, AA-safe as a button bg in both themes) and `--brand-accent` (text/icon/border — indigo-600 light / indigo-400 dark, AA-legible as small text in both). `--ring` points at the brand so focus rings are branded. Tailwind exposes `bg-brand` / `text-brand-foreground` / `text-brand-accent` (+ a `brand` Button variant). **Application is SELECTIVE**: brand goes on chrome the eye lands on (primary CTAs, active nav, header mark, hero, stat numbers, table heatmap/peak-highlight, focus ring); near-black `--primary` stays the workhorse for secondary/admin buttons. **Off-limits:** the `/notes` + `/guide` *content* colors (concept-card indigo, amber traps, sky self-check, violet practice, emerald checkpoints) are content semantics — do NOT fold them into the brand token or recolor them. See [[brand-indigo-tokens]] + the 2026-06-01 Decisions-log entry.
- **Icons.** Use `lucide-react` for all icons. Never emoji or text glyphs (▾, ‹, ›) for UI affordances. Standard sizes: `h-3.5 w-3.5` for inline-with-text, `h-4 w-4` for buttons, `h-5 w-5` for the AppHeader logo.
- **Toasts.** `import { toast } from "sonner"`. Fire on every async user-initiated success or failure (save, upload commit, export download, sign-out). Setting an inline `error` state is fine, but never *replace* a toast with it — both should fire so the user can't miss the result while scrolled.
- **AppHeader.** Every page wraps its `<main>` in `<><AppHeader />…</>`. Don't re-add ad-hoc back-links, theme toggles, or sign-out buttons on individual pages — theme lives in the `ThemeToggle` island in the header (visible to anon + authed), sign-out lives in the UserMenu popover (authed only).
- **No Prisma.** Supabase RLS is the security boundary — every read/write goes through `@supabase/supabase-js` so the user's JWT carries through. Reach for `createSupabaseAdminClient()` (service-role) only in server-only contexts (seeds, cross-org admin) — it bypasses RLS by design.
- **Migrations are append-only.** New SQL goes in a new `000N_<name>.sql`. Apply via Supabase MCP `apply_migration`. Never edit a previously-applied file.
- **`.mcp.json` is gitignored.** Holds Supabase + Vercel personal access tokens. Don't commit.
- **Idempotent seeds + uploads.** `scripts/seed.ts` and the upload commit both check existence before insert; re-running is safe.
- **Content hashing for upload dedup.** `content_hash = sha256(normalised question text + sorted options + answer)` plus a unique index on **`(org_id, exam_id, content_hash)`** (migration 0038 — **per-exam**: was org-wide `(org_id, content_hash)` until 2026-06-15). Re-uploading the same file inserts 0 rows; the SAME question may recur across exams (CDS ↔ NDA share UPSC GAT English questions — org-wide dedup silently dropped the CDS copy). `commitStaged` upserts on `org_id,exam_id,content_hash`; `applyMockSync`'s content_hash lookups are exam-scoped to match.
- **Text normalization at write boundaries.** Long-form user-authored text (question, context, solution, options) goes through `normalizeNewlines` in the upload parser and the edit validator. The DB never carries literal `\n` (backslash+n) past the boundary; math zones are preserved verbatim. Any new write path that accepts long-form text must call the helper before insert/update — otherwise the render layer can't distinguish "real line break" from "literal text".
- **OMML + math defaults via post-processing.** Two `docx`-library limitations land on the same workaround. (1) `ImportedXmlComponent` wraps imported XML in an `<undefined>` element Word rejects, so we emit placeholder runs and swap them for OMML after `Packer.toBuffer`. (2) The library doesn't expose `<m:mathPr>` for settings.xml, and Word's built-in math defaults (`defJc=centerGroup`, `wrapIndent=1440`, non-zero margins) shift any paragraph containing a 2-D math element (`<m:f>` etc.) ~1″ right of plain-text paragraphs — option `(A) -3/4` ends up out of column with option `(A) -1`. `patchZip` in `src/lib/export/docxBuilder.ts` handles both: marker → OMML in `word/document.xml`, and `<m:mathPr defJc=left wrapIndent=0 lMargin=0 rMargin=0>` injected before `</w:settings>` in `word/settings.xml`.
- **Restart `npm run dev` after adding new routes.** Tailwind JIT can miss files created mid-session. Stop dev, `rm -rf .next`, restart before UI smoke tests.
- **`/notes` LaTeX in body prose only.** Four `SubtopicNote` / `ConceptUnit` fields render as plain text (no KaTeX) AND feed SEO `metadata.description` / JSON-LD where KaTeX would be invalid: `note.title`, `note.oneLineDefinition`, `note.whyItMatters`, `concept.name`. LaTeX in any of these leaks through as raw `\(\hat{i}\)` markup on the page AND in page metadata. **Keep math only in body fields** (`intuition`, `definition`, `formula.latex`, `authoredExample.{prompt,steps,answer}`, `trap.{title,body}`) — those go through `KatexRenderer` correctly. Use Unicode (`î`, `ĵ`, `k̂`, `Σ`, `°`) or plain prose for the four plain-text fields. **Body fields also support `**bold**`** (renders via KatexRenderer / RichText — use it for key terms), and the **`definition` field additionally supports `- ` bullet lists** (rendered by `RichText`): bullet *list-shaped* definitions (term/type/property enumerations), keep narrative ones as prose. The plain-text fields render `**`/`- ` literally — never use them there.
- **Word export: lowercase option labels at render only.** Bank stores `A/B/C/D` uppercase (Excel + DB convention); the `.docx` export renders them as `(a)/(b)/(c)/(d)` in both question paper and answer key (`opt.label.toLowerCase()` in docxBuilder). Browse cards + edit form still show uppercase — only the rendered Word doc is lowercased. If a future surface needs to render bank-style labels, follow the same boundary: lowercase at render, never in the DB.
- **Word export: underline bypasses the math pipeline.** DB convention is `\(\underline{\text{word}}\)` (or `\(\underline{\textit{word}}\)` for italic+underline — biology taxonomy questions). KaTeX renders these in the browser; the docx export detects them via `UNDERLINE_BYPASS_RE` in [src/lib/export/ommlBuilder.ts](src/lib/export/ommlBuilder.ts) and emits a native Word run (`TextRun({ text, underline: { type: "single" }, italics? })`) instead of routing through `temml → mml2omml → <m:borderBox>` — Word doesn't reliably draw the bottom border on inline-math borderBox under our injected math defaults. Trailing punctuation INSIDE the delimiters (`\(\underline{\text{x}}.\)`) is split off into a separate text segment. Any pattern outside the regex (bare `\underline{word}`, chained `\text{}\text{}`, `\textbf` variants, block math) falls through to the OMML pipeline and renders without an underline in Word — run `npm run audit:underlines` after each new English / Biology batch to catch fall-throughs.
- **Tables in question content use GFM pipe-tables (2026-06-02).** A table inside a question `text` or `context` is authored as a standard Github-flavored markdown pipe-table — a header row **plus a `|---|---|` separator row**, then data rows. The separator row is mandatory and is what distinguishes a real table from inline `|·|` math (determinant `\(|A|\)`, abs value `|x|`, cell notation `Zn|Zn²⁺`): a line of pipes with **no** following separator stays prose. `parseTableBlocks` in [src/components/math/parseTableBlocks.ts](src/components/math/parseTableBlocks.ts) (pure, math-masked via `maskMathZones`, TDD in `tests/parse-table-blocks.test.ts`) splits text into prose + table blocks; cells keep their `\(...\)` math and `**bold**`. Rendered by `BlockText` (`src/components/math/BlockText.tsx`) on `/browse` (expanded card only — collapsed preview stays plain `KatexRenderer` so a table can't blow past `line-clamp-2`) + the editor Preview + the `/notes` mastery-check / `/guide` worked-example cards (`WorkedExampleCard`'s `example.context` + `example.text`, 2026-06-04), and as a native Word `<w:tbl>` in `docxBuilder` (the question NUMBER rides on the first paragraph; a stem opening with a table gets a numbered empty paragraph first). **No DB primitive** — it's all in the existing `text`/`context` columns; `normalizeNewlines` is compatible (it upgrades an Excel-authored literal-`\n` table into real newlines). **Future-only**: the existing 13 Statistics (lossless 2-row linearization) + 37 Match-Lists were NOT backfilled — they still render as linearized text. Options don't support tables (v1). For a genuine ≥3-column data grid that can't be a markdown table, fall back to a question image like a diagram.

## Recurring pitfalls

Patterns that have bitten this codebase more than once. Each one earned its place by causing a real production regression — read before writing new code that resembles the shape.

- **PostgREST 1000-row cap on raw `.select()`.** Any `client.from(...).select(...)` that derives a count, a distinct set, a sort, or any aggregate from the *row payload* (rather than response headers via `count: "exact"` or a SQL aggregate via RPC) is suspect once the table can exceed 1000 rows. PostgREST silently truncates at row 1000 — no error, just wrong answers. **Already bit us three times:** dashboard totalQuestions (migration `0018` get_dashboard_stats), PYQ-year filter dropping older years (migration `0019` get_pyq_years), and upload commit dedup races (fixed by switching to `.upsert(..., { onConflict, ignoreDuplicates: true })` — let the DB unique index enforce dedup, not an in-memory `Set`). **Audit checklist before writing any new `.select()`:** (1) Is the row payload itself the answer, or are you computing something from it? (2) If computing, can the underlying table exceed 1000 rows ever (today or as the bank grows)? (3) If yes, use `count: "exact", head: true` for counts, or write a `security invoker` RPC that does the aggregate in Postgres. Never trust a row-derived count from `.select()` past the first 1000 rows. **Bit us twice more 2026-06-11:** `quiz:lint` used a bare `.limit(2000)` which PostgREST still caps at 1000, so it silently scanned only 1000 of ~1762 ready atoms (fixed by paging in 1000-row `.range()` windows); and **the sibling self-imposed-limit trap** — `/dashboard/quizzes` `listAssembledQuizzes` had a hard `.limit(60)` and a "Quizzes built" stat derived from `list.length`, so once the table crossed 60 quizzes (now 109) the oldest silently vanished from the filtered admin view AND the stat under-reported. Rule: a hard `.limit(N)` on a list hides rows once the table exceeds N, and any stat from `list.length` lies — derive counts from a true `count:"exact"`, and raise/paginate the list.
- **`next dev` is more forgiving than `next start` for server↔client prop serialization.** Functions and other non-serializable values passed from a Server Component to a Client Component compile cleanly under `next dev` but throw at SSR time under `next start` ("Functions cannot be passed directly to Client Components"). This bit us on the 2026-05-13 Pagination revert. **Mitigation:** the pre-push hook in `.githooks/pre-push` runs `next build` before allowing a push, so this class of bug is caught locally rather than after a Vercel deploy. Don't `git push --no-verify` unless you know exactly why.
- **`next build` does NOT exercise a DYNAMIC (`ƒ`) page's render — so the build gate is blind to its render-time throws.** Only static/SSG (`○`/`●`) routes are prerendered at build; a server-rendered-on-demand page (any page that awaits cookies / a per-request query — most `/guide/*` + `/notes/*` pages that call `resolveTaxonomy` via the anon client) is never executed until a real request, so a render exception passes `typecheck` + `lint` + `next build` + the full pre-push gate and only 500s in production. **Bit us 2026-06-14:** the new `/guide/nda-physics/ncert-map` page named a `<NcertChip>` prop `ref` — React **reserves** `ref` (it's never passed to a plain function component, so the value was `undefined` at render → "Refs cannot be used in Server Components"). Typecheck passed because the prop was explicitly typed `{ ref: NcertRef }`; the build passed because the page is `ƒ`. **Two mitigations:** (1) never name a component prop `ref` (or `key`) — they're React-reserved regardless of your prop type; (2) **curl/browser smoke-test every new DYNAMIC route before claiming done** — the build gate's green is necessary but NOT sufficient for `ƒ` pages (it IS sufficient for `○`/`●` pages, which prerender). A data-only test (like the ncert-map taxonomy test) doesn't render the page, so it can't catch this either.
- **Source-source subtopic name collisions during bulk chapter merges.** When canonicalizing N source chapters into one target chapter, a bulk `UPDATE subtopics SET chapter_id = '<tgt>' WHERE chapter_id IN ('<src1>', '<src2>', ...)` can fail with `23505` on `subtopics_chapter_id_name_key` even if a pre-flight source-vs-target collision check came back clean — because two sources may share a same-named subtopic that collides only once both are reparented. Bit us on the NDA Physics E&M cluster (5 sources → "Magnetic Field Inside a Solenoid" appeared in two of them). **Mitigation:** process sources sequentially via a `DO $$ FOR mapping IN (VALUES (...)) LOOP ... END $$` block; each iteration redirects colliding questions, deletes colliding source subtopics, reparents the rest, updates remaining question chapter_ids, and deletes the source chapter — so by the time source 2 merges, source 1's content is already in target and the standard collision-handling kicks in. Pattern + full template in `[[reclassification-sql-pattern]]`.
- **Catch-all chapters and subtopics drift until you reclassify per-question.** Buckets named "Mechanics", "Physics", "Error Identification in Sentences" collect every question that doesn't cleanly fit a more-specific name. They look harmless during a chapter-level merge but rot the bank's usefulness for technique drills. **Mitigation:** when a chapter or subtopic holds ≤~50 mixed-topic questions, read each stem and produce a `(question_id, target_canonical_id)` mapping; execute as a `DO $$ FOR m IN (VALUES (qid, tgt)) LOOP ... END $$` that (a) looks up the question's source subtopic, (b) finds same-named subtopic in target chapter, (c) redirects+deletes on collision, otherwise reparents+moves chapter_id. Precedents: Spotting Errors 29 q (2026-05-15 NDA English); Mechanics 19 q + Physics 26 q catch-all chapters (2026-05-15 NDA Physics). Beyond ~50 q the user prefers chapter-level merge + subtopic-cleanup as a follow-up pass.
- **`/notes` subtopic slugs are GLOBALLY unique, not chapter-scoped.** `question_concept_tags` keys on `(question_id, subtopic_slug, concept_slug)` with **no chapter or subject column**, and `loadConceptDrills` / the mastery-checkpoint query filter by `subtopic_slug` ALONE. So two chapters that both use a slug like `foundations` will cross-contaminate each other's drill lists + checkpoints. Bit us 2026-05-30: MHT-CET Indefinite Integration reused Sound's `foundations` slug — notes-lint caught it as "DB tag has unknown concept_slug" because the other chapter's concepts don't match. **Mitigation:** every notes subtopic slug must be unique across ALL chapters in `NOTES_CHAPTERS` (the URL segment can repeat per subject route, but the slug used as the concept-tag key cannot). MHT-CET's foundation subtopic was renamed `foundations` → `fundamentals`. notes-lint surfaces the collision but only *after* tags are inserted — pick distinctive slugs up front.
- **Renaming a SHIPPED chapter silently rots its `/guide` + `/notes` cross-refs.** The DB taxonomy and the guide/notes editorial TS are joined only by NAME at request time (`resolveTaxonomy`), so a Phase-D rename/merge leaves the TS pointing at a dead subtopic — the "Drill the N →" CTA resolves to an EMPTY `/browse` filter, no error. Bit us twice on 2026-05-30: the Sound notes 5→4 redesign broke the `/guide/nda-physics` Sound playbook + strategy `mustDrill` + CHAPTER_TABLE prose; the 2026-05-29 Probability merge ("Probability with Dice" → "Probability via Counting") broke two `nda-maths` principle drills + the Probability tier. **Mitigation:** after any rename/merge of a shipped chapter, sync `/guide` `_data` (playbooks `subtopics[]`, strategy `mustDrill`/`skipSubtopics`, principles `drill`, compounds filters, prose counts) + `/notes` `_data` + concept tags, then run `npm test` — the 7 playbook guides + CA themes + Maths each have a `describe.skipIf(!HAS_ENV)` live-taxonomy resolution test (`guide-nda-<subject>-playbooks.test.ts`; `guide-nda-maths-taxonomy.test.ts` for Template A) that fails on drill-target drift. Full procedure: [[shipped-chapter-rename-downstream-sync]]. The `/browse` backlink chip (`getQuestionResources` name lookup) + the single-page Economics chapter CTA are the cross-link surfaces still untested.
- **Integration-test fixtures leak into the LIVE bank when `afterAll` is interrupted — and only `org_id` reliably catches them.** Every DB-integration test inserts throwaway rows (org, questions, taxonomy) into the single prod Supabase project and cleans them in `afterAll`; a parallel/killed/throwing run skips that cleanup, stranding PUBLIC fixtures on `/browse` and inflating the bank counts. Bit us 2026-06-11: 18 fixture questions (11 PUBLIC) + 12 orgs leaked. **No content signal is complete** — `source_file`+`upload_job_id` both NULL misses fixtures that set a test `upload_job_id`; text-pattern misses the `goodXlsxBuffer` upload-flow fixtures (realistic stems, **real sha256 `content_hash`, under canonical taxonomy**); `content_hash !~ '^[0-9a-f]{64}$'` (0 real rows) misses those same ones. **The one universal signal is `org_id`:** every fixture belongs to a throwaway org whose name carries an 8-hex run-id (`randomUUID().slice(0,8)`). **Mitigation:** `global-teardown` is now an org-keyed cascade sweep (`isTestOrgName`, [[test-data-leak-org-signal]]) — but it's a SAFETY NET that only runs at the end of a full `vitest run`. A subset/watch run that creates fixtures and is then killed leaves them until the next full suite. After any interrupted DB-test run, sweep manually or run a full `npm test`. To clean: clear `question_reports` (its `org_id` FK is RESTRICT) then delete the test orgs (CASCADEs questions→options/tags/reports + org_members + upload_jobs).

## Design axes

Load-bearing design distinctions for the project. Each one earns its place by being structural — getting the axis wrong has cost real work, and the answer to "which axis does this live on?" is non-obvious for the named feature areas.

### Tag taxonomies — principles (horizontal) vs concepts (vertical)

**`/guide` principles** and **`/notes` concepts** are different kinds of tag and must NEVER share a schema or be merged into one taxonomy.

| | Principles | Concepts |
|---|---|---|
| Axis | Horizontal — cross-subtopic | Vertical — within-subtopic |
| Cardinality | ~20 in NDA Maths | ~5–10 per subtopic × hundreds = thousands at scale |
| Storage | TS modules (`/guide/.../principles.ts`) + curated `extraQuestionIds` | DB table `question_concept_tags` (migration 0021) |
| Authoring | Editorial — hand-curated, slow update cadence | Per-upload — admin tagging UI or chat session, fast cadence |
| Value comes from | **Selectivity** (only the top-20, hand-picked) | **Coverage** (every relevant question tagged) |
| Identity question | "What unifying lens does this question demand?" | "What specific technique within this chapter?" |

**The wall — non-negotiable:**
- DON'T migrate `/guide` principles to a DB table. Hand-curation is the signal-to-noise mechanism; "scaling" them dilutes their value.
- DON'T bundle principles into `question_concept_tags` via a `tag_kind` discriminator. Flattens the strategic distinction.
- DO share infrastructure at the seam: one admin tagging UI on `/questions/[id]/edit`, one notes-lint validator, future LLM-suggest can suggest both kinds. Sharing UI ≠ sharing schema.

**Why this earned a section:** The initial concept-tags proposal (Phase 1, 2026-05-17) was a unified `question_tags` table. User pushed back; I conceded. Both axes shipped separately (principles still in TS, concepts in migration 0021) and the wall has held. This section exists so future tag-related features (admin UI, LLM-suggest, AI tutor grounding, MHT_CET_AI integration) don't re-litigate it from scratch.

### Cross-link backlinks — chapter-level (automatic name lookup) vs tag-level (manual DB-tagged override)

The `/browse` QuestionCard chip row (Tier 1 + Tier 1.5, 2026-05-18) renders **two distinct classes of backlink** and the wall between them matters. Same dropdown of chips, two completely different authoring loops and data sources.

| | Chapter-level chips | Tag-level chips |
|---|---|---|
| Examples | "Playbook: Sound" · "Concept notes" | "Lever: Vieta" · "Concept: Weighted Mean" |
| Source | Name-based lookup at render time | DB row in `question_principle_tags` / `question_concept_tags` |
| Authoring cost | **Zero per question** — purely a function of `(exam, subject, chapter, subtopic)` | **Manual per question** — chat tagging session, INSERT with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` |
| Coverage | 100% of questions whose chapter has a guide / subtopic has notes | Only questions explicitly tagged (today: 488 principle tags · 262 concept tags) |
| Failure mode | Silent break when DB chapter renamed (no lint catch yet — see ROADMAP "notes-lint guide-side rename validation") | Silent fallback to generic chip when slug doesn't resolve via `getPrincipleName` / `getConceptName` |
| Override behaviour | The default; renders for all qualifying questions | When present, **replaces** the chapter-level chip for the same axis (more specific wins) |
| Resolver | `getQuestionResources({exam, subject, chapter, subtopic})` in `src/lib/links/questionResources.ts` | `getQuestionResources({...}, tags)` — same function, second arg from `getResourceTagsForQuestions` batch |

**The wall — design intent:**
- DON'T expand the chapter-level lookup to do "fuzzy" matching when a chapter rename happens. The failure mode (chip disappears) is the correct signal — fix the rename, don't paper over the drift.
- DON'T attempt to back-fill tag-level chips from the chapter-level data (e.g. auto-create a `Lever: <chapter-name>` chip when no principle tag exists). That destroys the curated-vs-automatic distinction and floods low-leverage questions with meaningless tag chips.
- DO let tag-level chips quietly fall through to chapter-level chips when the tag's slug doesn't resolve. Better a generic "NDA Maths strategy" chip than a broken `/principles/<unknown-slug>` link.
- DO share infrastructure at the seam: both axes flow through the same `getQuestionResources(input, tags?)` function, both render via the same `<ResourceChip>` component in QuestionCard. The two-tier system is a function-signature distinction (optional second arg) not two separate code paths.

**Why this earned a section:** The 2026-05-18 Tier 1.5 ship added tag-level overrides AFTER the Tier 1 chapter-level chips were in production. The temptation in the moment was to refactor into "one unified backlink table" or to have tag-level chips deduplicate against chapter-level chips. Both ideas are wrong — they conflate two different authoring loops (curated tagging session vs no-touch render-time mapping) and two different coverage shapes (long-tail per-question vs blanket-per-chapter). The split shipped cleanly because the function signature preserved the asymmetry: `tags?` is optional, the resolver short-circuits chapter-level chips when a more-specific tag-chip exists, and untagged questions still get the generic chip — graceful fallback throughout. Sister wall to the tag-taxonomies axis above.

### Guide strand axis — execution-mode (chapter-partitioning) vs tier-style (chapter-tiering)

Within Template B variants, the **strand split** is the strategic axis the strategy page renders against. There are two fundamentally different ways to construct it, and they are NOT interchangeable — picking the wrong one produces a strategy axis that sorts questions by nothing meaningful.

| | Execution-mode strands | Tier-style strands |
|---|---|---|
| Subjects | NDA English (R/Rule/Reason), NDA Chemistry (R/Rule/Calculate), NDA Biology (R/Apply/Verify), NDA Geography (R/Apply/Verify) | NDA History (Cornerstone/Foundation Recall/Quick-Win) |
| Strand semantics | Each strand is a SKILL bucket (recall a fact / apply a rule / trace a mechanism / evaluate a statement). Chapters get grouped into a strand by their dominant execution mode. | Each strand is a STRATEGIC TIER (cornerstone weight / foundation recall / quick-win). Chapters get grouped into a strand by their bank share + %HARD profile. |
| Recall slot | Stable — there's always a Recall strand (named-fact recall is universal in Indian entrance exams). | Stable in spirit (Foundation Recall) but the slot SEMANTICS bent — it now groups chapters by tier, not by execution mode. |
| When to pick | Execution modes PARTITION chapters: one cluster of chapters is 60%+ dominated by one mode, others are 20%- on that mode. | Execution modes are UNIFORM across all chapters (25–45% everywhere) — can't partition. AND/OR one chapter has dominant bank share (40%+) that warrants its own Cornerstone tier. |
| Decision probe | For each candidate mode (recall/rule/apply/verify/calculate/reason/multi-statement/date-anchored), count `q × chapter_spread` AND per-chapter %. Top mode at 60%+ in one cluster = execution-mode strands work. | Top mode is 25–45% of every chapter = uniform = falls back to tier-style. Use bank-share + %HARD per chapter as the partition signal instead. |
| HARD calibration | Strand-level (densest-HARD strand called out — Geography's Apply at 23% avg HARD). | Implicit in the tier-structure — Cornerstone IS the densest-HARD tier by construction (History's Cornerstone = Modern India 34% HARD). |

**The wall — design intent:**
- DON'T force execution-mode strands when modes don't partition. History's multi-statement (70 q × 4 ch · 43% HARD) and date-anchored (61 q × 4 ch · 43% HARD) cut UNIFORMLY across chapters — a Bio/Geo-style R/Apply/Verify split would force lopsided cuts (Recall 44 q : Date 163 q : Verify 53 q if grouped by chapter's dominant mode). The strategy axis would sort by nothing meaningful.
- DON'T force tier-style strands when execution modes DO partition cleanly. English's Vocab/Idioms (Recall) and RC/Cloze (Reason) chapters are genuinely different SKILL types, not just different priorities — a Cornerstone/Foundation/Quick-Win split would lose that information.
- DO let the decision probe pick. Test execution-mode first (Template B default); fall back to tier-style only when modes are uniform.
- DO share infrastructure at the seam: both axes use the same `STRATEGY_STRANDS` data shape (each strand has `id`, `label`, `pitch`, `approach`, `chapters: StrandChapter[]`) and the same strategy-page renderer. The two-axis distinction is in the BUCKET MEANINGS (`PlaybookBucket = "recall" | "apply" | "verify"` for Bio/Geo vs `"cornerstone" | "foundation" | "quickwin"` for History) — same code path, different semantics.

**Why this earned a section:** The 2026-05-19 NDA History ship was the first time Template B's strand axis itself bent. Before History, every Template B variant had bent the second + third SLOTS within an execution-mode axis (Rule→Apply for Bio, Calculate→Verify for Chem) — but the axis itself was always execution-mode. History exposed the next level: when execution modes don't partition chapters at all, the axis itself has to change. The temptation in the moment was to force one of the existing slot-bend patterns onto History (most naturally R/Apply/Verify like Bio + Geo) — that would have shipped a strategy page where students see "Apply chapters" and "Verify chapters" labels but no chapter actually leans either way, sorting by nothing. The tier-style fallback shipped cleanly because the strand data shape was already general (chapters grouped under labelled strands with a pitch + approach) — only the bucket vocabulary changed. Sister wall to the tag-taxonomies + cross-link-backlinks axes above: in all three, the right call is to preserve an asymmetric distinction at the shared seam rather than to flatten for elegance.

## Guide structure templates

Three guide structures have shipped. They serve different bank shapes; a fourth subject's guide should pick from these (or pivot again) by analysing the bank first, not by copying precedent.

### Template A — principles-first (NDA Maths)

Use when the bank has **cross-chapter levers** (one principle spans ≥4 chapters) and **non-flat %HARD** (multiple chapters > 20% HARD).

- **Routes:** landing + `/strategy` (Tier A/B/Skip) + `/principles` (catalog) + `/principles/[slug]` (deep dives) + `/compound-tricks` + `/trends` + `/traps`.
- **Tag infra:** DB-backed `question_<X>_tags` table (today: `question_principle_tags`, migration 0023). Hand-curated TOP_N; counts + %HARD + chapter spread computed live from the tag table at request time.
- **Strategy axis:** %HARD-driven tiering. Chapters with high q-count AND low %HARD = Tier A; high q-count + high %HARD with one drillable subtopic = Tier B cherry-pick; everything else = skip.
- **Editorial data shape:** `OVERVIEW + CHAPTER_TABLE + TIER_A/B + SKIP_LIST + TOP_11 principles + DOMAINS long-tail + COMPOUNDS + DRIFT_ROWS + TRAPS + per-principle DETAILS`.

### Template B — playbooks-first (NDA English, NDA Chemistry, NDA Biology, NDA Geography, NDA History)

Use when the bank has **sealed chapters** (largest cross-chapter lever < ~30 q). Pure Template B also requires **flat %HARD** (≤2 chapters > 15%); the Geography variant relaxes that gate (see "B + non-flat %HARD" below).

- **Routes:** landing + `/strategy` (skill buckets, not tiers) + `/playbooks` (catalog) + `/playbooks/[slug]` (deep dives — one per major subtopic OR one per chapter — see variants below) + `/<subject-specific-page>` (e.g. `/vocab-families` for English, `/common-compounds` for Chemistry) + `/trends` + `/traps`.
- **Tag infra:** NONE. Cross-chapter horizontal axis is too thin to justify a DB table. Drill CTAs use `subtopicIds` or `chapterIds` from `resolveTaxonomy` at request time — same machinery as the maths long-tail DOMAINS principles.
- **Strategy axis:** Skill-type buckets, not %HARD tiers. The middle bucket is the variable slot — pick it from the subject's natural seams:
  - **English** uses Recall (Vocab + Idioms — memorisation) / **Rule** (Errors + Grammar — rule application) / **Reason** (RC + Cloze + Rearrangement + FIB — context-driven).
  - **Chemistry** uses Recall (Carbon + Matter + Industrial + Metals + Hydrogen + Everyday Life + Practical — fact recall) / **Rule** (Atomic Structure + Acids/Bases + Reactions + Bonding — rule application) / **Calculate** (Mole Concept — small numeric strand). Chemistry has no chain-reasoning lever (no analogue of RC author-stance) but does have a small but distinct numeric strand worth surfacing as its own bucket.
  - **Biology** uses Recall (Human Physiology + Cell Biology + Microbiology + Biodiversity + Genetics — named-fact recall) / **Apply** (Plant Biology + Reproduction — mechanism-tracing: photosynthesis flow, osmosis direction, inheritance ratios, pollination genetics) / **Verify** (Ecology + Biochemistry — multi-statement true/false evaluation, the bank's second-largest question shape at ~24 q). Biology has NO numeric strand (no analogue of Mole), no Rule-application lever beyond pattern-application (which collapses into Recall in practice), and only 4 chain-reasoning HARDs across 190 q — too thin for a Reason strand. The **third slot bent to Verify** to capture the dominant non-recall execution mode; the **second slot bent to Apply** to surface the mechanism-tracing chapters that carry 4 of the bank's 5 HARDs. So Biology's Recall + Apply + Verify split is unique even within Template B — the Rule slot itself bent.
  - **Geography** uses Recall (Indian Geography Economy + Indian Geography Physical + World/Human — named-fact recall: rivers/peaks/states/crops/minerals/ports) / **Apply** (Climatology + Earth's Structure — mechanism-tracing: cyclogenesis, plate tectonics, weathering, rock-cycle classification) / **Verify** (Earth in Space + Oceanography — multi-statement evaluation: "consider the following statements about terrestrial planets / cold ocean currents"). Same Recall/Apply/Verify split shape as Biology; the unique twist is that Geography has **non-flat %HARD** (4 of 7 chapters >15% HARD) — see "B + non-flat %HARD" below for the variant pattern. Initial guess was a Map/Concept/Current-Events split (in the deprecated "other subjects may need different splits" prose); SQL analysis showed Recall/Apply/Verify fits better because Geography's multi-statement shape (92 q) dominates the non-recall execution mode.
  - Current Affairs (not yet a guide) might still need a different split — one-bucket-many-themes. The Recall slot is universally stable; the OTHER TWO slots bend to the subject's natural seams.
- **Playbook granularity:** English uses per-subtopic playbooks (chapters are skill buckets, each subtopic is a distinct question type — 16 playbooks across 8 chapters). Chemistry uses per-chapter playbooks (subtopics well-cleaned but thin at 2–5 q each — 12 playbooks 1:1 with chapters, same rationale as physics). Biology uses per-chapter playbooks too (9 playbooks 1:1 with chapters; subtopics thin, max 13 q on HP.Circulatory + MD.Pathogens). Geography uses per-chapter playbooks too (7 playbooks 1:1 with chapters; subtopics avg ~5 q, max 36 q on Agriculture). Pick based on whether subtopics are large enough to anchor a standalone deep-dive.
- **Subject-specific page:** Required for Template B in practice. English's `/vocab-families` (13 themed word clusters); Chemistry's `/common-compounds` (6 themed name↔formula↔use clusters, ~50 compounds, rendered via the new `CommonCompoundsTable` component); Biology's `/reference-tables` (4 themed clusters, ~52 named-fact pairs — rendered via the generic **multi-domain** `ReferenceTables` component: each cluster carries its own column headers); Geography's `/reference-tables` (4 themed clusters, 62 named-fact pairs — Indian Rivers / Mountain Peaks / Mineral & Crop Producer States / Local Winds + Climate Zones — same generic `ReferenceTables` component). All four follow the same recall-anchor pattern — a flat-list reference the recall strand drills against.
- **Editorial data shape:** `OVERVIEW + CHAPTER_TABLE + STRATEGY_BUCKETS + PLAYBOOKS + per-playbook DETAILS + <subject-specific clusters/families> + DRIFT_ROWS + TRAP_SHAPES`.

#### Template B variants — pure-B strand axis (English / Chemistry / Biology)

Three subjects fit pure Template B (sealed chapters + flat %HARD) but differ on the strand split and on subject-specific page shape. Biology in particular shows that even the SECOND slot can bend (Rule → Apply) when the bank has no rule-application lever. Geography is NOT in this table — its strand split is identical to Biology's; what makes Geography different is its non-flat %HARD, covered in the second table below. Use this table as a quick decision aid for the next pure-Template-B subject — match each axis to the new subject's seam, don't reflexively copy any column.

| Axis                       | English (the original B)                                                                   | Chemistry (variant 1)                                                                                | Biology (variant 2)                                                                                  | What decided                                                                                         |
|----------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| **Bank size**              | 900 q · 8 ch · 29 sub                                                                      | 262 q · 12 ch · 47 sub                                                                               | 190 q · 9 ch · 39 sub                                                                                | Bank shape, not template choice.                                                                     |
| **Strand split**           | Recall / **Rule** / **Reason** — Vocab+Idioms · Errors+Grammar · RC+Cloze+Rearrangement+FIB | Recall / **Rule** / **Calculate** — Carbon+Matter+Industrial+Metals+Hydrogen+Everyday+Practical · Atomic+Acids+Reactions+Bonding · Mole | Recall / **Apply** / **Verify** — HP+Cell+Micro+Biodiversity+Genetics · Plant+Reproduction · Ecology+Biochemistry | Biology has no rule-application lever (the Rule slot itself bent → Apply) and no numeric strand (Calculate doesn't apply). |
| **Bucket shapes**          | Recall (memorise) / Rule (apply principle) / Reason (chain-context inference) | Recall (memorise) / Rule (apply principle) / Calculate (numeric plug-in) | Recall (memorise) / Apply (mechanism-trace) / Verify (multi-statement T/F evaluation) | What execution modes the bank's questions ACTUALLY demand.                                          |
| **% of bank**              | Recall 45% / Rule 25% / Reason 30%                                                          | Recall 55% / Rule 42% / Calculate 3%                                                                  | Recall 69% / Apply 22% / Verify 8% (by chapter grouping; by question shape Recall is 82%)             | Calculate is small (3%) because Mole is one chapter. Verify is small (8%) because Ecology + Biochemistry are the only chapters where the shape dominates. |
| **Playbook granularity**   | Per-subtopic (16 playbooks across 8 chapters) — chapters are skill buckets, subtopics are distinct question types large enough to anchor a deep-dive | Per-chapter (12 playbooks 1:1 with chapters) — subtopics well-cleaned but thin at 2–5 q each, would proliferate without adding clarity | Per-chapter (9 playbooks 1:1 with chapters) — subtopics thin (max 13 q), per-subtopic split would proliferate | Largest subtopic size. English's biggest is Synonyms 150 q; Chemistry's Allotropes 15 q; Biology's Circulatory 13 q. |
| **Subject-specific page**  | `/vocab-families` — 13 themed word clusters mined from PYQ underlines                       | `/common-compounds` — 6 themed name↔formula↔use clusters, ~50 compounds                              | `/reference-tables` — 4 themed clusters (diseases↔pathogens, vitamins↔deficiencies, hormones↔glands, scientists↔discoveries), ~52 named-fact pairs | The recall-strand's highest-leverage flat-list artefact. All three are 4-pass active-recall references.   |
| **Subject-specific renderer** | Inline JSX in the page (no dedicated component — clusters are simple `<ul>` lists)      | `CommonCompoundsTable` component (single-domain — every cluster is name↔formula↔use)             | Generic `ReferenceTables` component (MULTI-domain — each cluster carries its OWN `{name,fact,context}` column headers; Bio/Geo/History/Polity all pass their `REFERENCE_CLUSTERS` + `guidePath`) | Compound table needs richer per-row affordances than word lists; reference-tables need per-cluster column headers since the domains differ. |
| **Trends headline framing**| "Grammar exploded post-2024" — chapter-mix shifts are the story                            | "Paper has NOT hardened" — explicit negation of Physics's "hardened 22×" framing                     | "Paper has NOT hardened — only 4 HARDs across 190 q over 10 yrs" — even more explicit absence-of-drift framing (Biology is flatter than Chemistry) | Whether the bank's drift has a sharp story or a stable one. Frame the absence of drift explicitly when a reader might import the wrong calibration from a sibling guide. |
| **Trap-page bucketing**    | 9 trap shapes by Recall/Rule/Reason — meaning-based (near-synonym, near-idiom, near-passage-inference) | 12 trap shapes by Recall/Rule/Calculate — identity-confusion (acid-source swap, diamond-graphite property flip, scientist-discovery swap) | 13 trap shapes by Recall/Apply/Verify — paired-fact-swap (disease↔pathogen, vitamin↔deficiency, hormone↔gland, RNA-vs-DNA virus, scientist↔discovery) + apply-flips + multi-statement traps | Distractor genre. English: semantic. Chemistry: identity. Biology: paired-fact. Physics: formula misapplication.            |
| **DriftTable `rowLabel`**  | `"Chapter"`                                                                                 | `"Chapter"`                                                                                          | `"Chapter"`                                                                                          | All three flow chapter-rows (vs Maths' principle-rows). Shared.                                          |
| **Related-playbooks renderer** | Generic `RelatedPlaybooks` — call site passes `guidePath="nda-english"` + `items={PLAYBOOKS}`                           | same generic, `guidePath="nda-chemistry"`                                          | same generic, `guidePath="nda-biology"`                                             | Consolidated 2026-06-01 — the 8 former per-guide copies differed only by an import + one href, so they collapsed to one parameterised component (the "don't parameterise until forced" call was reversed once the duplication hit 8). |

**The strand-axis lesson (updated by Biology + Geography + History):** ONLY the Recall slot is universally stable across Template B subjects — and even that can manifest as one strand (Recall) or part of a larger Foundation Recall tier. The SECOND slot is usually Rule but can bend to Apply (Biology + Geography mechanism-tracing) or — when even execution-mode is uniform across chapters — to TIER (Cornerstone for History's dominant Modern India). The THIRD slot is the most variable — Reason (English chain-context), Calculate (Chemistry numeric Mole strand), Verify (Biology + Geography multi-statement evaluation), Quick-Win (History's low-%HARD World History). Picking the strand axis itself (execution mode vs strategic tier) is the highest-leverage decision in a new Template B guide. **History was the first subject where execution-mode strands failed** — multi-statement and date-anchored modes are uniform across all 4 chapters, so they couldn't partition. Tier-style strands (Cornerstone / Foundation Recall / Quick-Win) were the fallback.

#### Template B variants — strand-axis fork (execution-mode vs tier-style)

| Strand axis | Subjects | When to pick | Examples |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Execution-mode strands** | English, Chemistry, Biology, Geography | When execution modes (Recall/Rule/Reason etc.) partition chapters into recognisable clusters — each chapter has a DOMINANT execution mode that distinguishes it from others. | English: Vocab/Idioms = Recall vs RC/Cloze = Reason. Bio: HP/Cell = Recall vs Plant/Reproduction mechanism-tracing = Apply. |
| **Tier-style strands** | History, Polity | When execution modes are bank-wide-UNIFORM (cut across all chapters at similar %), OR the bank is too small + chapter count too low for skill-bucket labels to be more actionable than strategic-priority labels. | History: multi-statement 70 q × 4 ch + date-anchored 61 q × 4 ch are uniform — but Modern India is 47% of bank (vs next chapter 20%), creating a Cornerstone/Foundation/Quick-Win priority axis. Polity: only 90 q · 4 ch · avg 5 q/paper (smallest GAT section); multi-statement partition is partial (4.5–50% across 4 chapters); strategic-priority labels name-drop each chapter directly. |

**Decision probe:** For each candidate execution mode, count `q × chapter_spread` AND look at the per-chapter distribution. If the top mode is 60%+ of one chapter and 20%- of others, execution-mode strands work. If it's 25–45% of every chapter, modes are uniform → fall back to tier-style. **Also drop to tier-style** when the bank is small enough (≤100 q · ≤4 ch) that strategic-priority labels are more actionable than execution-mode labels at this scale — Polity's call.

**Tier-style third-tier inversion** (History vs Polity): tier-style strands have a stable Cornerstone (dominant chapter) + Foundation Recall (recall-heavy mid-chapters) — but the THIRD tier's semantics depend on the smallest chapter's profile. History's World History is small AND easiest (20% HARD, lowest in subject) → **Quick-Win** label. Polity's World Polity is small BUT HARDEST (42% HARD, highest in subject, 50% multi-statement) → **Specialist Wildcard** label (small + high-reward, opposite framing). Don't reach reflexively for "Quick-Win" — verify the third tier's %HARD profile against the rest of the subject first, and relabel when the smallest chapter is the HARDEST.

#### Template B variants — %HARD-handling axis (all 6 B subjects)

A separate axis from strand split: how does each Template B variant deal with its %HARD shape? Three of the four early B subjects were flat (no special handling needed); Geography forced the discovery of a non-flat-%HARD fork; History sharpened the case for the tier-style strand-axis fork (above); Polity added a second tier-style subject with INVERTED third-tier semantics.

| Axis                                  | English                                          | Chemistry                                            | Biology                                             | Geography                                                                                                                                                       | History                                                                                                                                                          | Polity                                                                                                                                              | What decided                                                                                                                                                       |
|---------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| **%HARD spread**                      | flat (1 ch >15%: Sentence Rearrangement 22%)     | flat (0 ch >12%; max 11.1% Mole)                     | flat (max 7.7% on 13-q chapter; 4 ch at 0% HARD)   | **non-flat** (4 of 7 ch >15%: Climatology 28% / IG Economy 24% / Earth's Structure 20% / Earth in Space 18%)                                                     | **non-flat** (ALL 4 ch >15%: Modern 34% / Medieval 28% / Ancient 27% / World 20%)                                                                              | **borderline non-flat** (only 1 of 4 ch clearly >15%: World 42% outlier; other 3 cluster 13.6 / 15.0 / 16.7%)                                       | The pure-B vs B-variant fork. Geography + History fail the "≤2 chapters >15% HARD" gate; Polity is borderline but the outlier is small + immovable.                  |
| **HARD concentration within chapters**| n/a (flat)                                       | n/a (flat)                                           | n/a (flat)                                          | **diffuse** (Climatology HARD 4-3-3-3-2-1 across 6 subs; Earth's Structure 5-4-3-2-1-0-0 — no chapter has 1–2 subtopics carrying all the HARD)                  | **mixed-immovable** (Modern's top-2 share 63% LOOKS concentrated, but the top sub is Freedom Movement 56 q · 46% of chapter — the chapter's PRIMARY content, cannot be skipped) | **small-and-immovable** (World Polity's HARDs concentrate in UN 60% + Democracy 40% — both 5-q subs that ARE the chapter)                          | The B-variant vs Template-C fork. Concentrated + skippable → Template C (Physics). Diffuse OR concentrated-but-immovable → Template B + non-flat variant.            |
| **%HARD handling on the strategy page**| none needed                                      | none needed                                          | none needed                                         | strand-level calibration — densest-HARD strand (Apply at 23% avg) called out in hero subtitle; no per-chapter posture cards                                      | Cornerstone strand IS the densest-HARD strand (Modern 34% HARD = 1 chapter strand) — strand calibration is implicit in the tier-strand structure                | Specialist Wildcard strand IS the densest-HARD strand (World 42% HARD = 1 chapter strand) — strand calibration is implicit in the tier-strand structure | Strand-level calibration carries the non-flat HARD signal without per-chapter DrillPosture. For History + Polity, the tier-style strand structure already encodes it. |
| **Per-chapter `DrillPosture` overlay?** | no                                              | no                                                   | no                                                  | **no** (despite non-flat %HARD)                                                                                                                                  | **no** (despite non-flat %HARD AND apparent concentration — but the concentrated pool is immovable)                                                              | **no** (only 1 ch clearly non-flat AND the chapter is too small — 12 q — for DrillPosture to add signal)                                            | DrillPosture only earns its place when HARD is BOTH non-flat AND concentrated AND skippable. Geography fails the concentration test; History fails the skippable test; Polity fails the bank-size test. |
| **Strand axis**                       | execution-mode (Recall/Rule/Reason)              | execution-mode (Recall/Rule/Calculate)               | execution-mode (Recall/Apply/Verify)                | execution-mode (Recall/Apply/Verify)                                                                                                                             | **tier-style (Cornerstone/Foundation Recall/Quick-Win)** — execution modes were uniform across chapters, forcing the fallback                                    | **tier-style (Cornerstone/Foundation Recall/Specialist Wildcard)** — execution modes partition only partially (4.5–50%) AND bank is small (90 q · 4 ch); strategic-priority labels more actionable. Third tier INVERTED from History's pattern (smallest chapter is HARDEST not easiest) | When execution modes partition chapters → execution-mode strands. When uniform OR bank is small enough that strategic-priority labels are more actionable → tier-style. |
| **Trends headline framing**           | "Grammar exploded post-2024" — chapter-mix shifts | "Paper has NOT hardened" — explicit negation of Physics's 22× framing | "Paper has NOT hardened — only 4 HARDs across 190 q over 10 yrs" | "Paper has NOT consistently hardened — %HARD bounces 6%→42% with no monotonic trajectory; 2018 + 2025 outlier highs" | "Paper has NOT consistently hardened — %HARD bounces 19%→42%→10%, 2021 peak, 2026-1 lowest" + secondary callout "chapter mix shifted: Modern dominated 2017–20, Ancient surged 2022–24 from ~2 q/yr to 9–10 q/yr" | "Paper has NOT consistently hardened — %HARD bounces 0%→50% with noise inherent to small per-paper samples (avg 5 q/paper)" + secondary callouts "2026 NDA-1 was hardest + most Govt-Structure-heavy 7-of-10; 2021 FR/DPSP outlier 7-of-14"   | Frame the absence/shape of drift explicitly when a reader might import the wrong calibration from a sibling guide. Polity's noise floor is amplified by the small per-paper sample. |

**The %HARD-handling lesson:** Template B's "flat %HARD" gate is the most easily relaxed prerequisite. When %HARD is non-flat but HARD is **diffuse within chapters**, stay in Template B and switch from "no HARD signal" to "strand-level HARD calibration" — don't escalate to Template C. The escalation to C only earns its place when HARD pools are concentrated enough that "drill all EASY subs, skip the HARD ones" is a meaningful piece of strategy.

### Template C — chapter-playbooks + skill-strand + formula compendium (NDA Physics)

Use when **neither A nor B fits cleanly**: cross-chapter lever too thin for principles (max < ~30 q × ≥4 ch — fails A's gate) AND %HARD is mid-spread (3–6 chapters > 15% HARD — too non-flat for B's strategy to capture). NDA Physics: max cross-chapter lever 12 q × 6 ch; 5 of 14 chapters > 15% HARD with HARD concentrated in 1–2 specific subtopics per chapter (Resistance & Circuits 42%, Calorimetry 31%, Thermo Processes 33%, Buoyancy 31%, etc.).

- **Routes:** landing + `/strategy` (skill strands with %HARD-aware DrillPosture overlay) + `/playbooks` (catalog) + `/playbooks/[slug]` (deep dives — **one per chapter**, not per subtopic; physics chapters ARE the natural unit, subtopics too fine at 1–3 q each) + `/formulas` (single-page compendium — NEW page kind) + `/trends` + `/traps`.
- **Tag infra:** NONE (same rationale as B). Per-playbook drills use `chapterIds` + optional `subtopicIds` from `resolveTaxonomy` at request time.
- **Strategy axis:** Skill strand (Recall / Apply / Reason) PLUS per-chapter `DrillPosture` ∈ {drill-all, drill-all-target-hard, cherry-pick-easy-mod, skim}. The posture answers the question B can't: "drill every subtopic of this chapter, or cherry-pick the EASY/MOD subtopics and skip the HARD ones?" — necessary when 5 chapters carry real HARD load.
- **Editorial data shape:** `OVERVIEW + CHAPTER_TABLE + STRATEGY_STRANDS + (per strand) StrandChapter[] with DrillPosture + targetHard + skipSubtopics + PLAYBOOKS (1 per chapter) + per-playbook DETAILS + FORMULA_GROUPS (chapter-grouped) + DRIFT_ROWS + HARD_BY_YEAR + DRIFT_CALLOUTS + TRAP_SHAPES (by strand)`.
- **New components:** `FormulaSheet` (chapter-grouped formula renderer — plain-text + unicode, no KaTeX pipeline → page stays server component). Related-playbooks use the generic `RelatedPlaybooks` component (`guidePath="nda-physics"`).

### Single-page landing (NDA Economics)

Use when the bank is **too thin for any multi-route template** to produce a non-parody strategy. NDA Economics: 24 q · 1 chapter · 3 subtopics · 1.5 q/paper · ~6 marks/paper max — Template A impossible (1 chapter rules out cross-chapter levers), Template B/C `STRATEGY_HEADLINE` reads as parody ("attempt 1.5 of 1.5, target 4 of 6"). Deliberately thinner terminal node, not a fourth template variant.

- **Routes:** landing only. No `/strategy`, no `/playbooks`, no `/trends`, no `/traps`, no `/principles`, no `/reference-tables`, no per-playbook detail pages.
- **Tag infra:** NONE. Cross-chapter axis impossible at 1 chapter.
- **Strategy stance:** named in prose, not in a `STRATEGY_HEADLINE` constant. Cap the subject explicitly ("~6 marks of 600-mark GAT, ~1%"), tell the reader what to do (drill the bank, internalise the one recall anchor, accept the cap), and link out.
- **Editorial data shape:** `OVERVIEW + SUBTOPIC_TABLE + <single-domain recall anchor>` (e.g. `FIVE_YEAR_PLANS` for Economics) `+ POST_PLAN_NOTE` or analogue. No `CHAPTER_TABLE` — 1 chapter doesn't warrant a table.
- **New components:** NONE. The subject's recall anchor renders as an inline `<table>` on the landing — simpler than `CommonCompoundsTable` because there's no per-cluster sub-grouping.
- **Sitemap priority 0.7** (vs 0.8 for full landings) — flags the thinness for crawlers.
- **`questionResources.ts` maps the subject's chapter to the landing** (Template A pattern — single landing, label `"NDA <subject> strategy"`) so `/browse` cards still get a backlink chip.

The bank-thinness gate is the entry point — see "Choosing the template" step 0.

### Template D — theme-prep checklist for short-half-life subjects (NDA Current Affairs)

Use when the bank's CONTENT goes stale (specific facts rarely repeat across years) BUT the bank's SHAPES are stable (recurring question types persist even when the specific facts in them rotate). NDA Current Affairs: 180 q · 8 ch · 31 sub · ~10 q/paper · ~40 max marks — multi-route templates would teach students 2017–2025 trivia and call it strategy. Template D is the honest alternative: name the half-life, name the recurring shapes, point at this year's news.

- **The half-life test** (empirical entry criterion): SQL-probe the bank for "year mentioned in question vs paper year". If 80%+ of explicit-year mentions are within 12 months of paper, half-life is short. Cross-check by counting recurrence of named entities (specific exercises / schemes / award winners) across paper-years — if each named entity appears in at most one paper, fact-memorisation has near-zero forward-prep value.
- **The stable-themes counter-test:** before defaulting to no-ship, count subtopics that appear in 5+ years of papers. If ≥6 subtopics meet that threshold and carry ≥40% of bank, the recurring SHAPES are stable enough to anchor a guide even when the FACTS aren't.
- **Strand axis = recurrence strength** (NEW — not skill, not strategic priority): Anchor themes (5+ year recurrence) → Recurring themes (3–4 year recurrence) → Occasional themes (1–2 year recurrence). Strand sizes are uneven by design; the prep-investment asymmetry is the point.
- **Theme cards** (the load-bearing artefact) — each anchor theme card carries: (a) **question SHAPE** — one sentence describing the recurring stem pattern, sourced from real bank stems (durable); (b) **prep CHECKLIST** — 3–6 fact-CATEGORIES to harvest from this year's news (durable directives, the student fills in this year's specific facts from an external compendium); (c) optional **durable-anchors** callout — the small subset of structural facts that genuinely don't rotate (G7 nations, UN agency HQs, ISRO launch-vehicle classes, etc.); (d) **drill BrowseLink** — practises the SHAPE against the bank's historical instances, NOT the facts.
- **Routes:** landing ONLY. No /strategy, no /playbooks, no /trends, no /traps, no /reference-tables, no per-playbook detail pages. Every section lives on the single page, ordered: hero + half-life directive + chapter table + anchor theme cards + recurring themes table + occasional themes chips + test-day plan + drill CTAs + closing rationale.
- **Tag infra:** NONE. Cross-chapter axis impossible — each chapter is a different subject domain (Defence ≠ Sports ≠ Awards). No `question_<X>_tags` table.
- **New components:** NONE. Anchor theme cards render as inline JSX `<article>` (same pattern as nda-economics's inline FYP `<table>`).
- **Editorial data shape:** `OVERVIEW + CHAPTER_TABLE + ANCHOR_THEMES (8 entries with shape + checklist + durableAnchors? + drillSubtopics) + RECURRING_THEMES (16 entries with oneLineShape + drillSubtopics) + OCCASIONAL_THEMES (7 entries minimal) + HALF_LIFE_NOTICE + TEST_DAY_PLAN`.
- **STRATEGY_HEADLINE stance:** named in the TEST_DAY_PLAN block, not in a copy-pasted constant. CA per-paper count is ~10 q (range 6–14), so ~40 max marks and target ~24 marks at 80% on 7 attempts.
- **Sitemap priority 0.6** (vs 0.7 for Economics, 0.8 for full landings) — flags the unusual template shape for crawlers.
- **`questionResources.ts` maps the subject's chapters to the landing** (Template A / single-page pattern — single landing, label `"NDA <subject> strategy"`).
- **Honest framing in copy is non-negotiable** — the half-life directive callout at the top of the page MUST explicitly say "drill this bank for SHAPE, not for FACTS" and point at an external compendium. Without it, students mistake the bank for the prep resource and memorise historical answers. The "Why this guide is one page" closing section names the alternative we didn't ship ("a 5-section guide would teach you 2019 facts and call it strategy").

### Shared across all guide treatments

- `GuideShell`, `GuideSideNav`, `GuideHero`, `StatBlock`, `BrowseLink`, `PrevNextNav`, `ChapterCard`, `WorkedExampleCard`, `GuideJsonLd` are all generic. The single-page landing treatment skips `GuideShell` + `GuideSideNav` + `PrevNextNav` (no sub-routes to navigate) but still uses `GuideHero` + `StatBlock` + `BrowseLink` + `GuideJsonLd`. Threading new behaviour through them as props (the 2026-05-18 ship added `landingHref` on Shell/SideNav, `rowLabel` on DriftTable) is the right pattern. Don't fork components per guide.
- `_data/*.ts` modules and the route topology are subject-specific. Diverge there.
- `/sitemap.ts`, Footer link, and `/browse` Hero pill plumbing additions are mandatory per new guide.
- Cross-link banner from the parent subject's landing (e.g. nda-maths landing → nda-english) is mandatory to discover the new guide.
- **Operations — before shipping `STRATEGY_HEADLINE`:** verify per-paper q-count against the live bank. Each PART B section has its own q-count (Physics ~25, Chemistry ~15, Biology ~10–11); copy-pasting between sibling guides has shipped wrong numbers (Chem + Bio caught 2026-05-18). The full SQL probe + 7-step recalibration recipe lives in [[per-paper-q-counts]] memory. `tests/guide-strategy-headline.test.ts` codifies the invariants — `paperQ × marksPerCorrect === totalMarks` (unit) and `paperQ within ±30% of live-bank avg q/paper` (DB integration). Run `npm test -- guide-strategy-headline` before commit.

### Choosing the template — the analysis script

Before proposing a new guide, run this analysis (SQL probes documented in [[english-guide-structure-diverges]] memory):

0. **Bank-thinness gate.** If avg q/paper < 2 OR total bank < ~50 q OR chapters = 1, no Template A/B/C will produce a non-parody `STRATEGY_HEADLINE`. **Ship a single-page landing instead** (see "Single-page landing" section above). NDA Economics (24 q · 1 ch · 1.5 q/paper · ~6 max marks) is the precedent; NDA Polity (90 q · 4 ch · 5 q/paper · 20 max marks) is the smallest *multi-route* guide and clears the floor. Below that floor, multi-route padding hurts more than it helps.
0a. **Content half-life gate** (run AFTER the thinness gate clears). SQL-probe `regexp_matches(text, '(20[12][0-9])', 'g')` against the bank and compute `(year_mentioned - paper_year)`. If 80%+ of explicit-year mentions are within 12 months of paper AND specific named entities (exercises / schemes / awards) almost never recur across paper-years, the content half-life is too short for a multi-route fact-memorise guide. Counter-test: if ≥6 subtopics appear in 5+ years (stable SHAPES even when FACTS rotate), **ship Template D — Theme-Prep Checklist** (see section above). NDA Current Affairs (180 q · 8 ch · 10 q/paper · 90% within-12-months mention rate · ~zero entity recurrence · 8 anchor themes at 5+ year recurrence) is the precedent.
1. **Chapter mix + %HARD per chapter.** Count chapters at each %HARD tier (>15%, >20%, >25%). Helps separate flat (Template B) from non-flat (A or C).
2. **Cross-chapter principle candidates.** Run keyword surveys for plausible horizontal levers. If the top candidate spans ≥4 chapters AND ≥40 q, **principles axis lives — Template A**. If it spans <4 chapters AND <30 q, **principles axis dead — go to step 3**.
3. **Pick B vs C by %HARD spread + concentration.** Three branches:
   - **≤2 chapters > 15% HARD** → flat — **pure Template B** (English / Chemistry / Biology shapes).
